    "newString": "  \\item \\textbf{Domain ordering invariance}: the hierarchy arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4/Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) is preserved across six models spanning four architecture families, three scales (1B--14B), and four decoding strategies; code (Python) matches web text despite a different modality (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nWeb-scale text corpora are increasingly contaminated by synthetic content that has itself been generated from earlier synthetic content.\nEstimates suggest that a substantial fraction of online text is machine-generated~\\cite{sun2025aigt, spennemann2025quantifying}, and this share continues to rise.\nWhen models train on the outputs of earlier models, the resulting multi-generational synthetic text degrades with each iteration: tail modes vanish, lexical diversity contracts, and surprisal distributions flatten~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, dohmatob2024model}.\nThis degradation arises in both \\emph{retrain-chains} (iteratively training on model outputs) and \\emph{prompt-chains} (recursively generating from previous outputs without retraining); we study the latter, which isolates generation-level information loss from training dynamics (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nYet the dominant paradigm treats all machine-generated text identically, discarding the generational depth dimension.\n\nThis gap cuts across multiple communities.\nBinary detectors~\\cite{basani2026diveye, zhang2024minkpp} produce no depth estimate (Section~\\ref{sec:binary_confidence}).\nMembership inference~\\cite{zawalski2026codec} addresses a different question, and the model collapse literature~\\cite{shumailov2024collapse, alemohammad2024selfconsuming, schaeffer2025position} provides no per-text tool.\nData selection methods~\\cite{xie2023dsir, tirumala2023d4, drayson2025} likewise ignore recursive depth.\nNo existing method estimates how many rounds of recursive generation a text has undergone.\n\nKnowing whether the discriminable depth is finite, and where it saturates, has direct practical consequences.\nFor data curation, the saturation point determines filtering granularity: if discrimination collapses at depth 2, graduated weighting beyond that boundary injects noise, and binary filtering becomes the principled choice.\nModel collapse theory predicts monotonic quality degradation across generations, but existing analyses are population-level and provide no per-text measure of where degradation saturates.\n$\\Kempirical$ fills this gap by localizing the generation at which adjacent outputs become empirically indistinguishable.\n\nThe Data Processing Inequality implies that information degrades monotonically across generations, motivating a finite discrimination boundary $\\Kstar$.\nWe empirically measure $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which a classifier reliably distinguishes adjacent generations) using 15 statistical features computed via a fixed reference scorer (Section~\\ref{sec:features}) and an Ordinal Binary Decomposition (OBD) LightGBM classifier.\nWe operationalize $\\Kempirical$ as the deepest $k \\geq 1$ where the pairwise $\\auc_{k-1,k}$ exceeds $\\theta = 0.60$ (Section~\\ref{sec:formulation}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}[label=(\\alph*),nosep]\n  \\item \\textbf{First empirical measurement of $\\Kempirical$} across 9 model configurations (5 architecture families, 1B--14B), 4 source domains (3 text + 1 code), and 4 decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$: 4/5 families (8/9 configurations) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on C4 ($p \\leq 0.002$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}).\n  Both supervised OBD and zero-shot frontier Large Language Models (LLMs) show no statistically significant difference beyond $\\Kempirical$ (McNemar $p > 0.05$, $N{=}500$; Section~\\ref{sec:why_kstar}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Domain ordering invariance}: the hierarchy arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) is preserved across six models spanning four architecture families, three scales (1B--14B), and four decoding strategies, with $\\Kempirical$ consistently varying by source domain (Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}).\n  \\item \\textbf{Practical validation}: largely generator-independent features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention among tested continuation-mode configurations ($\\rho > 0.90$; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}), and $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary filtering as optimal granularity for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",

{

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Domain as the primary factor.}\nWith the scorer confound controlled, the domain ordering is uniform across all tested models: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$).\nNeither model scale (1B--14B), architecture family, nor decoding strategy alters this ordering; source-domain lexical diversity is the primary determinant of $\\Kempirical$.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$): cross-modality confirmation.}\nPython functions from CodeSearchNet~\\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$, Qwen2.5-1.5B) yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.738$, CI $[0.709, 0.767]$; 2v3 $\\auc = 0.530$; Table~\\ref{tab:code_domain_qwen} in Appendix).\nDespite a different modality, code matches C4 web text ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), with higher 1v2 AUC (0.74 vs.\\ 0.61--0.70) but the same sharp 2v3 collapse (JSD ratio 1v2/2v3 $= 11.7\\times$), indicating that syntactic constraints provide stronger per-step signal without extending the boundary.\n\n\\paragraph{Domain as the primary factor.}\nWith the scorer confound controlled, the domain ordering is uniform across all tested models: arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4/Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$).\nNeither model scale (1B--14B), architecture family, nor modality (natural language vs.\\ code) alters this ordering; source-domain structural complexity is the primary determinant of $\\Kempirical$."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 web text yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), and Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent domain ordering that correlates with lexical diversity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nThe only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\\Delta = -1.8$\\,pp, $\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 web text yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), Python code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.74$; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent ordering that correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nCode matching C4 despite a different modality indicates that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by domain structure rather than input type; syntactic constraints provide stronger per-step signal (higher 1v2 AUC) without extending the boundary.\nThe only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\\Delta = -1.8$\\,pp, $\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\\ref{sec:latex_ablation})."
}
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01K4annNjTDf5tNrXwY34L1Q`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
19	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
20	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
21	Baseline classifier substitution (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) yields non-significant McNemar differences in 31/36 comparisons (Table~\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).
22	
23	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
24	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$); cross-model consistency indicates the contraction rate is domain-governed.
25	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects model-adaptation artifacts: $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Table~\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
26	
27	\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}
28	For web text, 2v3 $\auc < \theta$ across all configurations, so depth assignments beyond $d{=}2$ approximate random labels and graduated weighting injects noise (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
29	The strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with $\Kempirical{=}2$; on arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement, consistent with $\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.
30	
31	\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}
32	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent (frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$ vs.\ $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo; Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring reduces Wikipedia $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}, while preserving C4 feature rankings $\rho > 0.90$ and $\Kempirical$ for 6/8 configurations, Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
33	
34	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}
35	C4 web text yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), arXiv yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families; Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}), and Wikipedia yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent domain ordering that correlates with lexical diversity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
36	The only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\Delta = -1.8$\,pp, $\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).
37	
38	\paragraph{Surprisal attenuation and boundary variation.}
39	\label{sec:surprisal_attenuation}
40	Feature-level analysis reveals that the Mistral-7B exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$) arises from compressed surprisal dynamic range (depth-0 mean surprisal 2.32 vs.\ 2.90--3.02 for $\Kempirical{=}2$ models; mean $|\text{Cohen's}\;d| = 0.112$, the weakest; Table~\ref{tab:cohens_d_full}).
41	Across six C4 configurations, larger 0$\to$1 surprisal drops correlate with higher 1v2 $\auc$ (Table~\ref{tab:borderline_features}), because generators that better approximate the training distribution produce less per-step degradation; this attenuation mechanism predicts that $\Kempirical$ may decrease as capability scales beyond 14B (Qwen-14B still yields $\Kempirical{=}2$, $\auc = 0.621$).
42	
43	\paragraph{Why $\Kempirical$ matters.}
44	For curation, $\Kempirical{=}2$ validates binary filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) and provides post-hoc grounding for the binary approach of \citet{drayson2025}.
45	
```

Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage[section]{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*_{\\text{empirical}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ (the maximum depth at which adjacent generations remain distinguishable) using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nAcross five architecture families (1B--14B), four source domains (three text, one code), and four decoding strategies at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (AUC decision threshold), $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for general web text (8/9 model configurations) and code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for structured academic text (arXiv).\nA single additional generation pass suffices to render first-generation synthetic web text indistinguishable from deeper synthetic content, a \\emph{one-wash laundering} effect.\nLargely generator-independent statistical features, computed via a fixed reference scorer, enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention among continuation-mode generators, and cross-scorer validation confirms that $\\Kempirical$ is largely robust to scorer choice.\nDownstream, $\\Kempirical$ characterizes discriminability boundaries that inform, but do not directly determine, filtering strategy; for web text ($\\Kempirical{=}2$), binary filtering aligns with the two-level discriminability structure.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\clearpage\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: model scale $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}500$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B represents the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignment, with the 1v2 CI lower bound within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); neural probe $\\Kempirical$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$), indicating the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

For the domain dependence discussion, I'm tightening the logic: the fact that code replicates C4's behavior across different input types demonstrates that K* is fundamentally driven by domain structure rather than modality itself.

Now looking at the domain ordering across models—the pattern holds consistently with arXiv at K*=3, C4 and Code both at K*=2, and Wikipedia at K*=1, independent of model scale or other confounds.

For the curation implications, K*=2 for web text directly validates the binary filtering strategy and aligns with prior work on this approach.

Looking at the conclusion, I can tighten it by consolidating the key empirical findings—web text shows K*=2 across most configurations, code yields K*=2, and arXiv gives K*=3—while noting that generator-independent features support binary filtering as the optimal granularity. The prompt-chain caveat can stay brief in the appendix reference.

C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nBaseline classifier substitution (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) yields non-significant McNemar differences in 31/36 comparisons (Table~\\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nToken-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); cross-model consistency indicates the contraction rate is domain-governed.\nThe retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects model-adaptation artifacts: $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Table~\\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}\nFor web text, 2v3 $\\auc < \\theta$ across all configurations, so depth assignments beyond $d{=}2$ approximate random labels and graduated weighting injects noise (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nThe strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement, consistent with $\\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent (frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo; Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring reduces Wikipedia $\\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}, while preserving C4 feature rankings $\\rho > 0.90$ and $\\Kempirical$ for 6/8 configurations, Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 web text yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), Python code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.74$; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent ordering that correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nCode matching C4 despite a different modality indicates that $\\Kempirical$ is governed by domain structure rather than input type; syntactic constraints provide stronger per-step signal (higher 1v2 AUC) without extending the boundary.\nThe only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\\Delta = -1.8$\\,pp, $\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surprisal attenuation and boundary variation.}\n\\label{sec:surprisal_attenuation}\nFeature-level analysis reveals that the Mistral-7B exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) arises from compressed surprisal dynamic range (depth-0 mean surprisal 2.32 vs.\\ 2.90--3.02 for $\\Kempirical{=}2$ models; mean $|\\text{Cohen's}\\;d| = 0.112$, the weakest; Table~\\ref{tab:cohens_d_full}).\nAcross six C4 configurations, larger 0$\\to$1 surprisal drops correlate with higher 1v2 $\\auc$ (Table~\\ref{tab:borderline_features}), because generators that better approximate the training distribution produce less per-step degradation; this attenuation mechanism predicts that $\\Kempirical$ may decrease as capability scales beyond 14B (Qwen-14B still yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, $\\auc = 0.621$).\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical$ matters.}\nFor curation, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ validates binary filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) and provides post-hoc grounding for the binary approach of \\citet{drayson2025}.\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01AMk8GS7uyxtD42dLzf6kaR`</summary>

```text
124	
125	The preceding experiments use C4 (general web text).
126	To test whether $\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we evaluate on arXiv abstracts ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model) and Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$ per model) across six model configurations spanning four architecture families (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).
127	
128	\paragraph{arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}
129	All five domain-competent models yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, with boundary-pair $\auc$ (2v3) ranging from 0.6215 to 0.6545, all exceeding $\theta$.
130	The elevated signal reflects higher per-generation distributional shift (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4): formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, mathematical notation) degrade slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth signal across four architecture families and 1.4B--8B parameters.
131	Removing LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\sim}$1.8\,pp but preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610 > \theta$; Table~\ref{tab:latex_ablation}); content-level properties dominate.
132	OLMo-1B constitutes a capacity failure rather than a counter-example; its arXiv generations are entirely out-of-domain (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$, near-total information loss at the first step), yielding $\Kempirical{=}1$.
133	Mistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence: $\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 (Section~\ref{sec:boundary_cases}) but $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv: domain structure alone can shift $\Kempirical$ by two levels within the same model.
134	
135	\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}
136	With scorer confound controlled (Pythia-1.4B for Qwen generators; Qwen-1.5B, itself cross-family, for all others), all six models yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ on Wikipedia: 1v2 $\auc$ ranges from 0.499 (OLMo) to 0.595 (LLaMA), all below $\theta$.
137	Wikipedia represents the simplest domain tested; its encyclopedic regularity provides minimal structural signal beyond the 0v1 boundary, and a single generation pass suffices for complete saturation.
138	A same-family scorer confound inflates the apparent $\Kempirical$ for Qwen generators: Qwen-1.5B scoring yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\auc \approx 0.61$--$0.62$), but cross-family scoring reduces 1v2 by 2.4\,pp to below $\theta$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).
139	This +1 depth bias under same-family scoring is specific to Qwen generators on Wikipedia and does not affect C4 results (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
140	
141	\paragraph{Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$): cross-modality confirmation.}
142	Python functions from CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$, Qwen2.5-1.5B) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.738$, CI $[0.709, 0.767]$; 2v3 $\auc = 0.530$; Table~\ref{tab:code_domain_qwen} in Appendix), matching C4 despite a different modality: higher 1v2 AUC (0.74 vs.\ 0.61--0.70) but the same sharp 2v3 collapse (JSD ratio $11.7\times$).
143	
144	\paragraph{Domain as the primary factor.}
145	With the scorer confound controlled, the domain ordering is uniform: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4/Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$).
146	Neither scale (1B--14B), architecture, nor modality alters this ordering; domain structural complexity determines $\Kempirical$.
147	
148	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
149	\centering
150	\footnotesize
151	\captionof{table}{Domain $\times$ Model $\Kempirical$ matrix (scorer-controlled). Wikipedia values use cross-family scorer (Pythia-1.4B). $^*$OLMo arXiv: capacity failure. $^\dagger$Same-family scorer yields $\Kempirical{=}2$; controlled value shown.}
152	\label{tab:domain_kstar}
153	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
154	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
155	\toprule
156	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{C4} & \textbf{Code} & \textbf{arXiv} & \textbf{Wikipedia} \\
157	\midrule
158	Qwen-1.5B    & 2 & 2 & 3 & 1$^\dagger$ \\
159	Qwen-7B      & 2 & --- & 3 & 1$^\dagger$ \\
160	Pythia-1.4B  & 2 & --- & 3 & 1 \\
161	OLMo-1B      & 2 & --- & 1$^*$ & 1 \\
162	LLaMA-3.1-8B & 2 & --- & 3 & 1 \\
163	Mistral-7B   & 1 & --- & 3 & 1 \\
164	\bottomrule
165	\end{tabular}}
166	\end{minipage}
167	
168	%% ====================================================================
169	\subsection{Binary Detector Confidence}
170	\label{sec:binary_confidence}
171	
172	Binary detectors (DivEye~\cite{basani2026diveye}, Binoculars~\cite{hans2024binoculars}) show moderate rank correlations with depth ($|\rho| = 0.06$--$0.46$; Table~\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}) but fail as depth proxies: 6-class accuracy is at or below chance ($\leq 0.199$ vs.\ 0.167 baseline), pairwise $\auc$ yields $\Kempirical^{\text{binary}} \leq 1$, and multiple conditions exhibit non-monotonic AUC at deeper pairs.
173	The observed correlation is an artifact of 0v1 separation rather than fine-grained depth signal.
```

[Director 指令] [补充] Code domain 入稿叙事细化

Reviewer 审查通过了 code domain 数据，额外提供了一个有价值的叙事点：

**JSD cliff 位置差异**：Code 和 C4 都是 K*=2，但 JSD 骤降位置不同：
- Code: 1v2→2v3 骤降 11.7x（信号在 depth 2 才 collapse）
- C4: 0v1→1v2 就骤降 9.8x（信号在 depth 1 就大幅衰减）

原因：code 的句法约束（缩进、括号配对、关键字频率）使 depth-1 文本保留更多可辨别信号。

**请在入稿时包含这个 "same K*, different dynamics" 的讨论**——它展示了 domain structure 不仅决定 K* 值，还影响信号衰减模式，为 "domain-governed K*" claim 增添层次。

**Retrain-chain framing 建议**（等 CI 完成后再入稿，但先预知 framing）：
- Prompt-chain K* = "wild detection"（检测者不知道文本来源）
- Retrain-chain K* = "model archaeology"（知道训练 pipeline）
- 两者互补，不矛盾

1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$.
19	A single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.
20	Converging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\ OBD; Table~\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).
21	Baseline classifier substitution (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) yields non-significant McNemar differences in 31/36 comparisons (Table~\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).
22	
23	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
24	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$); cross-model consistency indicates the contraction rate is domain-governed.
25	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects model-adaptation artifacts: $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Table~\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
26	
27	\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}
28	For web text, 2v3 $\auc < \theta$ across all configurations, so depth assignments beyond $d{=}2$ approximate random labels and graduated weighting injects noise (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
29	The strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with $\Kempirical{=}2$; on arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement, consistent with $\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.
30	
31	\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}
32	$\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent (frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 3$ vs.\ $\Kempirical^{\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo; Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring reduces Wikipedia $\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}, while preserving C4 feature rankings $\rho > 0.90$ and $\Kempirical$ for 6/8 configurations, Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
33	
34	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}
35	C4 web text yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), Python code yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.74$; Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent ordering that correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
36	Code matching C4 despite a different modality confirms that $\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure, not input type.
37	The only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\Delta = -1.8$\,pp, $\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).
38	
39	\paragraph{Surprisal attenuation and boundary variation.}
40	\label{sec:surprisal_attenuation}
41	Feature-level analysis reveals that the Mistral-7B exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$) arises from compressed surprisal dynamic range (depth-0 mean surprisal 2.32 vs.\ 2.90--3.02 for $\Kempirical{=}2$ models; mean $|\text{Cohen's}\;d| = 0.112$, the weakest; Table~\ref{tab:cohens_d_full}).
42	Across six C4 configurations, larger 0$\to$1 surprisal drops correlate with higher 1v2 $\auc$ (Table~\ref{tab:borderline_features}), because generators that better approximate the training distribution produce less per-step degradation; this attenuation mechanism predicts that $\Kempirical$ may decrease as capability scales beyond 14B (Qwen-14B still yields $\Kempirical{=}2$, $\auc = 0.621$).
43	
44	\paragraph{Why $\Kempirical$ matters.}
45	For curation, $\Kempirical{=}2$ validates binary filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) and provides post-hoc grounding for the binary approach of \citet{drayson2025}.
46

141	\paragraph{Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$): cross-modality confirmation.}
142	Python functions from CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$, Qwen2.5-1.5B) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.738$, CI $[0.709, 0.767]$; 2v3 $\auc = 0.530$; Table~\ref{tab:code_domain_qwen} in Appendix), matching C4 despite a different modality: higher 1v2 AUC (0.74 vs.\ 0.61--0.70) but the same sharp 2v3 collapse (JSD ratio $11.7\times$).

144	\paragraph{Domain as the primary factor.}
145	With the scorer confound controlled, the domain ordering is uniform: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4/Code ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$).
146	Neither scale (1B--14B), architecture, nor modality alters this ordering; domain structural complexity determines $\Kempirical$.

Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nBaseline classifier substitution (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) yields non-significant McNemar differences in 31/36 comparisons (Table~\\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nToken-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); cross-model consistency indicates the contraction rate is domain-governed.\nThe retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects model-adaptation artifacts: $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Table~\\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}\nFor web text, 2v3 $\\auc < \\theta$ across all configurations, so depth assignments beyond $d{=}2$ approximate random labels and graduated weighting injects noise (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nThe strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement, consistent with $\\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent (frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo;     "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nBaseline classifier substitution (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) yields non-significant McNemar differences in 31/36 comparisons (Table~\\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nToken-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); cross-model consistency indicates the contraction rate is domain-governed.\nThe retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects model-adaptation artifacts: $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Table~\\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}\nFor web text, 2v3 $\\auc < \\theta$ across all configurations, so depth assignments beyond $d{=}2$ approximate random labels and graduated weighting injects noise (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nThe strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement, consistent with $\\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent (frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo; Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring reduces Wikipedia $\\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}, while preserving C4 feature rankings $\\rho > 0.90$ and $\\Kempirical$ for 6/8 configurations, Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 web text yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), Python code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.74$; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent ordering that correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nCode and C4 share $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but with shifted cliff positions: C4 collapses at 0v1$\\to$1v2 ($9.8\\times$ JSD drop), while code collapses at 1v2$\\to$2v3 ($11.7\\times$) because syntactic constraints (indentation, bracket matching, keyword frequencies) preserve depth-1 signal; domain structure thus governs not only $\\Kempirical$ but also the decay profile.\nThe only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\\Delta = -1.8$\\,pp, $\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surprisal attenuation and boundary variation.}\n\\label{sec:surprisal_attenuation}\nFeature-level analysis reveals that the Mistral-7B exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) arises from compressed surprisal dynamic range (depth-0 mean surprisal 2.32 vs.\\ 2.90--3.02 for $\\Kempirical{=}2$ models; mean $|\\text{Cohen's}\\;d| = 0.112$, the weakest; Table~\\ref{tab:cohens_d_full}).\nAcross six C4 configurations, larger 0$\\to$1 surprisal drops correlate with higher 1v2 $\\auc$ (Table~\\ref{tab:borderline_features}), because generators that better approximate the training distribution produce less per-step degradation; this attenuation mechanism predicts that $\\Kempirical$ may decrease as capability scales beyond 14B (Qwen-14B still yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$, $\\auc = 0.621$).\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical$ matters.}\nFor curation, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ validates binary filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) and provides post-hoc grounding for the binary approach of \\citet{drayson2025}.\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01V5CNe53WRxAGZUzAYviJf3`</summary>

```text
34	\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\Kempirical$.}
35	C4 web text yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), Python code yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\auc = 0.74$; Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv yields $\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia yields $\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent ordering that correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
36	Code and C4 share $\Kempirical{=}2$ but with shifted cliff positions: C4 collapses at 0v1$\to$1v2 ($9.8\times$ JSD drop), while code collapses at 1v2$\to$2v3 ($11.7\times$) because syntactic constraints (indentation, bracket matching, keyword frequencies) preserve depth-1 signal; domain structure thus governs not only $\Kempirical$ but also the decay profile.
37	The only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\Delta = -1.8$\,pp, $\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).
38	
39	\paragraph{Surprisal attenuation and boundary variation.}
40	\label{sec:surprisal_attenuation}
41	The Mistral-7B exception ($\Kempirical{=}1$) arises from compressed surprisal dynamic range (depth-0 mean surprisal 2.32 vs.\ 2.90--3.02 for $\Kempirical{=}2$ models; mean $|\text{Cohen's}\;d| = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:cohens_d_full}).
42	Larger 0$\to$1 surprisal drops correlate with higher 1v2 $\auc$ across six C4 configurations (Table~\ref{tab:borderline_features}); this attenuation predicts that $\Kempirical$ may decrease as capability scales beyond 14B (Qwen-14B: $\Kempirical{=}2$, $\auc = 0.621$).
43	
44	\paragraph{Why $\Kempirical$ matters.}
45	For curation, $\Kempirical{=}2$ validates binary filtering for web text (Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) and provides post-hoc grounding for the binary approach of \citet{drayson2025}.
```

[Director 指令] [Patch] Retrain-chain K*=7 结果入稿（D077）。

**数据更新**：
- 原：retrain-chain K*≥4（depth 0-4 only）
- 新：retrain-chain K*=7（Qwen-1.5B LoRA, depth 0-7 完整）

**完整 AUC 表**：
| Pair | AUC | 95% CI (5-fold CV) |
|------|-----|---------------------|
| 0v1 | 0.9837 | [0.9821, 0.9854] |
| 1v2 | 0.8971 | [0.8949, 0.8993] |
| 2v3 | 0.8098 | [0.8052, 0.8144] |
| 3v4 | 0.7600 | [0.7528, 0.7672] |
| 4v5 | 0.7459 | [0.7379, 0.7538] |
| 5v6 | 0.7017 | [0.6958, 0.7076] |
| 6v7 | 0.6410 | [0.6376, 0.6445] |

**关键叙事点**：
1. K*=7 vs prompt-chain K*=2 的 3.5x 差距：LoRA 每代引入累积 model artifacts，使各代高度可区分
2. AUC 衰减加速（Δ从-0.014到-0.061），外推 7v8≈0.57 < θ → K*=7 大概率是 ceiling
3. Prompt-chain K*=2 是 "operational boundary for data curation"（text-intrinsic information loss），retrain-chain K*=7 是 "model archaeology" 上界
4. 两家族验证：Qwen K*=7 + Pythia K*≥4

**需要更新的位置**：
- Section 4.x retrain-chain 段落：K*≥4 → K*=7，加完整 7-pair 数据
- Appendix retrain-chain 表：扩展到 depth 7
- Abstract/Conclusion：如果提及 retrain-chain，从 K*≥4 更新为 K*=7
- Discussion: "prompt-chain as conservative lower bound" 的论证从 3.5x gap 更有力

CI 方法标注 "5-fold CV estimate"（与主 pipeline bootstrap 区分）。这次不用验证引用。

36	\emph{Prompt-chain versus retrain-chain.}
37	Retrain-chain validation (Pythia-1.4B LoRA, 5 generations) yields $\Kempirical{\geq}4$ (Table~\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\Kempirical{=}2$.
38	All pairwise CIs have lower bounds well above $\theta{=}0.60$ ($p < 0.001$), statistically confirming $\Kempirical{\geq}4$.
39	The elevated signal arises from cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (vocabulary narrowing, repetition bias) rather than text-intrinsic degradation; prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ is the operationally relevant boundary for data curation.
40	
41	\begin{table}[h]
42	\centering
43	\small
44	\caption{Retrain-chain pairwise $\auc$ with fold-based 95\% CIs (5-fold CV estimate; Pythia-1.4B LoRA, 5 generations). All CI lower bounds $\gg \theta{=}0.60$. Unlike prompt-chain experiments, retrain-chain CIs use fold-level estimates because bootstrap resampling would break the GroupKFold document structure (see Section~\ref{sec:retrain_ci_note}).}
45	\label{tab:retrain_ci}
46	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
47	\toprule
48	\textbf{Pair} & $\auc$ & \textbf{95\% CI} & $p$ \\
49	\midrule
50	0v1 & 0.9842 & $[0.9823,\,0.9860]$ & ${<}0.001$ \\
51	1v2 & 0.9471 & $[0.9432,\,0.9509]$ & ${<}0.001$ \\
52	2v3 & 0.8888 & $[0.8832,\,0.8943]$ & ${<}0.001$ \\
53	3v4 & 0.7994 & $[0.7920,\,0.8067]$ & ${<}0.001$ \\
54	\bottomrule
55	\end{tabular}
56	\end{table}
57	
58	\paragraph{CI methodology note.}
59	\label{sec:retrain_ci_note}
60	The main pipeline reports bootstrap 95\% CIs (1{,}000 or 10{,}000 resamples), which are valid because each bootstrap sample preserves the GroupKFold document structure: resampling operates on documents, and all depths of a given document remain together.
61	In the retrain-chain setting, each generation's training corpus is the \emph{output} of the previous generation's fine-tuned model, creating inter-generation dependencies that standard bootstrap resampling cannot preserve without inflating AUC estimates.
62	We therefore report fold-based 95\% CIs for retrain-chain experiments, computed as $\bar{x} \pm 1.96 \cdot s / \sqrt{K}$ from the $K{=}5$ GroupKFold AUC values.
63	
64	\emph{Threshold sensitivity.}
65	Table~\ref{tab:theta_sensitivity} reports $\Kempirical$ across $\theta \in [0.55, 0.70]$ for representative configurations including arXiv and cross-scorer conditions.
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$.\nA single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from multiply recycled content, limiting depth-aware curation to a binary boundary.\nConverging evidence supports a data-intrinsic interpretation: OBD achieves 96\\% depth-0 recall but only 14--22\\% for depths 2--4; frontier LLMs show 8--38\\% (McNemar $p > 0.05$ vs.\\ OBD; Table~\\ref{tab:s3_llm_zeroshot}).\nBaseline classifier substitution (logistic regression, linear SVC, random forest) yields non-significant McNemar differences in 31/36 comparisons (Table~\\ref{tab:mcnemar_obd_baselines}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nToken-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); cross-model consistency indicates the contraction rate is domain-governed.\nThe retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects model-adaptation artifacts: $\\Kempirical{\\geq}4$ (Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Table~\\ref{tab:retrain_ci}), far exceeding prompt-chain $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\n\n\\paragraph{Graduated filtering failure.}\nFor web text, 2v3 $\\auc < \\theta$ across all configurations, so depth assignments beyond $d{=}2$ approximate random labels and graduated weighting injects noise (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nThe strategy ordering binary $>$ graduated aligns with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; on arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$), graduated filtering similarly shows no improvement, consistent with $\\Kempirical$ capturing discriminability rather than curation utility.\n\n\\paragraph{Representation and scorer dependence.}\n$\\Kempirical$ is representation-dependent (frozen Qwen embeddings yield $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 3$ vs.\\ $\\Kempirical^{\\text{neural}} = 2$ for Pythia and OLMo; Table~\\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring reduces Wikipedia $\\Kempirical$ from 2 to 1, Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}, while preserving C4 feature rankings $\\rho > 0.90$ and $\\Kempirical$ for 6/8 configurations, Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain dependence of $\\Kempirical$.}\nC4 web text yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations, 1B--14B), Python code yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (1v2 $\\auc = 0.74$; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv yields $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia yields $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with scorer confound controlled (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}), producing a consistent ordering that correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nCode and C4 share $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but with shifted cliff positions: C4 collapses at 0v1$\\to$1v2 ($9.8\\times$ JSD drop), while code collapses at 1v2$\\to$2v3 ($11.7\\times$) because syntactic constraints (indentation, bracket matching, keyword frequencies) preserve depth-1 signal; domain structure thus governs not only $\\Kempirical$ but also the decay profile.\nThe only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$) has $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, reinforcing the domain-governed interpretation (Appendix~\\ref{sec:boundary_extended}); a LaTeX markup ablation confirms content-level properties dominate (2v3 $\\Delta = -1.8$\\,pp, $\\Kempirical$ unchanged; Appendix~\\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).\n\n\\paragraph{Surprisal attenuation and boundary variation.}\n\\label{sec:surprisal_attenuation}\nThe Mistral-7B exception ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) arises from compressed surprisal dynamic range (depth-0 mean surprisal 2.32 vs.\\ 2.90--3.02 for $\\Kempirical{=}2$ models; mean $|\\text{Cohen's}\\;d| = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:cohens_d_full}).\nLarger 0$\\to$1 surprisal drops correlate with higher 1v2 $\\auc$ across six C4 configurations (Table~\\ref{tab:borderline_features}); this attenuation predicts that $\\Kempirical$ may decrease as capability scales beyond 14B (Qwen-14B: $\\Kempirical{=}2$, $\\auc = 0.621$).\n\n\\paragraph{Why $\\Kempirical$ matters.}\nFor curation, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ validates binary filtering for web text (Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) and provides post-hoc grounding for the binary approach of \\citet{drayson2025}.\n",
